        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 1 — Foundation schema + code shape

## Overview

Land the additive schema (5 migrations: `0020`–`0024`) and the code skeletons (`TurnContext` fields, `BotSpec` carrier fields, `app/services/routing.py`, `MEDIATOR_BOT` DB-populated fields) called for in §16.1 of `docs/multi-agent-architecture.md`. **Zero NOT NULL constraints**, no call-site changes, no read of new columns — subsequent sprints flip switches on this foundation.

The repo is a Python (asyncpg) service with raw `.sql` migrations under `migrations/` (next free number is `0020`; `0019_feedback_reaction_context.sql` exists untracked and must not be touched). Working tree has unrelated dirty files (S1 brief lists them) — leave them alone. Mediator code lives in `app/bots/{base,mediator,registry}.py`; per-turn state in `app/services/turn_context.py`; existing identities in `users.phone` (NOT NULL UNIQUE since `0001_init.sql`) plus `users.onboarding_state` (added in `0005_plan6_ops.sql`).

Two locked inputs (§16.8) are inputs, not questions: legacy phones backfill as `transport='legacy'`; existing `out_of_bounds` rows all get an `artifact_topics` row pointing at the seeded `relationship` topic.

**Critical data-shape note.** `users.phone` stores both real phone numbers and Discord user IDs (see `app/services/discord.py:68` `_discord_user_id` stripping a `discord:` prefix, and `discord.py:179-191, 402-407` passing the stripped value to `upsert_user(phone=...)`). The locked §16.8 decision absorbs this: ALL existing rows backfill as `transport='legacy'` regardless of whether the value is a phone number or a Discord ID. The executor must audit the column's mixed semantics and confirm the backfill covers every row, not skip rows that "look like" Discord IDs.

DDL exact shapes (column names, types, partial-index predicates, the `bot_bindings`/`topic_status` `(user_id IS NOT NULL) <> (dyad_id IS NOT NULL)` XOR check) are taken verbatim from §2/§3/§7/§12 of the architecture doc. Polymorphic `artifact_table` text column on `artifact_topics`, no FK to artifact tables — orphan prevention is application discipline, lint comes in S2a.

## Phase 1: Schema migrations

### Step 1: Snapshot prod schema for rollback compare
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `pg_dump --schema-only "$DATABASE_URL" > backups/pre_s1_schema.sql` against the staging clone (caller-driven; staging URL not in scope of this plan). Commit the file under `backups/` for diffability.
2. **Audit** `users.phone` semantics: run `SELECT phone, count(*) FROM users GROUP BY phone` on the staging clone. The column is NOT NULL UNIQUE per `0001_init.sql:10`, so every user has exactly one value. Some rows contain phone numbers; some contain raw Discord user IDs (stripped of a `discord:` prefix at insert time by `_discord_user_id` in `discord.py:68`). Per the locked §16.8 decision, ALL rows backfill as `transport='legacy'`. Confirm the audit query returns the expected count and the executor understands the mixed semantics. Document findings in `backups/pre_s1_audit.txt`.

### Step 2: Migration `0020_topics_bots_bindings.sql` (`migrations/0020_topics_bots_bindings.sql`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Wrap** the whole file in `BEGIN; ... COMMIT;` matching `0001_init.sql` style; use `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` and `DO $$ ... EXCEPTION WHEN duplicate_object` for policy idempotency, also matching the repo pattern.
2. **Create** `topics(id uuid PK DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(), slug text NOT NULL UNIQUE, title text NOT NULL, created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now())`.
3. **Create** `bots(id text PK, display_name text NOT NULL, created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now())`.
4. **Create** `dyads`, `dyad_members`, `bot_bindings`, `channels`, `user_identities` exactly per §3/§12 DDL:
   - `bot_bindings`: `CHECK ((user_id IS NOT NULL) <> (dyad_id IS NOT NULL))` XOR constraint.
   - `channels`: `bot_id text NOT NULL REFERENCES bots(id)`, `UNIQUE (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)`.
   - `user_identities`: PK `(transport, address)`, `user_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id)`.
5. **Seed** (idempotent via `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`):
   - One `topics` row `(slug='relationship', title='Relationship')`.
   - One `bots` row `(id='mediator', display_name='Mediator')`.
   - Channel rows: create the `channels` table, but do NOT seed channel rows from the SQL migration. Channel identifiers are runtime configuration (env-vars `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`, `WHATSAPP_PHONE_NUMBER_ID`, etc.) and differ between staging and production. Instead, create a runtime bootstrap script `scripts/seed_channels.py` (see Step 2b) that reads env vars and upserts rows into `channels`. The 0020 migration only creates the table.
   - **Dyad seeding**: use conditional logic — only seed the dyad and dyad_members if `(SELECT count(*) FROM users) = 2`. If the count is 0 (empty CI test DB) or > 2, emit `RAISE NOTICE` with the count and skip seeding. Do NOT raise an exception. This keeps the migration CI-safe.
     ```sql
     DO $$
     DECLARE
       _user_count int;
       _dyad_id uuid;
       _user_ids uuid[];
     BEGIN
       SELECT count(*) INTO _user_count FROM users;
       IF _user_count = 2 THEN
         SELECT array_agg(id ORDER BY created_at) INTO _user_ids FROM users;
         INSERT INTO dyads (id) VALUES (gen_random_uuid()) ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING id INTO _dyad_id;
         INSERT INTO dyad_members (dyad_id, user_id) VALUES (_dyad_id, _user_ids[1]), (_dyad_id, _user_ids[2]) ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING;
         INSERT INTO bot_bindings (id, bot_id, dyad_id) VALUES (gen_random_uuid(), 'mediator', _dyad_id) ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING;
       ELSE
         RAISE NOTICE 'skipping dyad seed: user count is %, expected 2', _user_count;
       END IF;
     END $$;
     ```
6. **Backfill** `user_identities` from `users.phone`:
   ```sql
   INSERT INTO user_identities (user_id, transport, address, created_at)
   SELECT id, 'legacy', phone, now() FROM users WHERE phone IS NOT NULL
   ON CONFLICT (transport, address) DO NOTHING;
   ```
7. **Enable RLS + anon-deny** on every new table mirroring `0001_init.sql` lines 174–257. Use the `DO $$ ... EXCEPTION WHEN duplicate_object` pattern with `FOR ALL TO anon USING (false) WITH CHECK (false)`.

### Step 2b: Channel seeding bootstrap script (`scripts/seed_channels.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `scripts/seed_channels.py` — an asyncpg script that reads env vars (`DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`, `WHATSAPP_PHONE_NUMBER_ID`, etc.) and upserts channel rows into the `channels` table:
   ```python
   # For Discord: bot_id='mediator', transport='discord', address=<bot user id from token>
   # For WhatsApp: bot_id='mediator', transport='whatsapp', address=<phone number id>
   INSERT INTO channels (id, bot_id, transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)
   VALUES (...) ON CONFLICT (transport, address, COALESCE(guild_id,''), COALESCE(channel_id,'')) DO NOTHING;
   ```
2. **Document** in `scripts/README.md` that this script must be run manually after 0020 migration is applied and before any inbound traffic routes through new channels.

### Step 3: Migration `0021_artifact_topics.sql` (`migrations/0021_artifact_topics.sql`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `artifact_topics` per §2 DDL: PK `(artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id)`, status default `'active'` with CHECK `IN ('active','retired')`, FKs to `topics(id)` and `bots(id)` (for `tagged_by_bot_id`), `reason text`, `retired_at timestamptz`. Both partial indexes filtered on `status='active'`:
   - `CREATE INDEX ON artifact_topics (topic_id, artifact_table) WHERE status='active';`
   - `CREATE INDEX ON artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id) WHERE status='active';`
2. **Enable RLS + anon-deny**.

### Step 4: Migration `0022_topic_status_user_bot_state.sql` (`migrations/0022_topic_status_user_bot_state.sql`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `topic_status` per §7 DDL: XOR check `((user_id IS NOT NULL) <> (dyad_id IS NOT NULL))`, both partial unique indexes (`topic_status_user_key` on `(topic_id, user_id) WHERE user_id IS NOT NULL`, `topic_status_dyad_key` on `(topic_id, dyad_id) WHERE dyad_id IS NOT NULL`). Columns: `headline text NOT NULL`, `body text NOT NULL`, `last_updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()`, `last_updated_by_bot_id text NOT NULL REFERENCES bots(id)`.
2. **Create** `user_bot_state(user_id uuid REFERENCES users(id), bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id), onboarding_state text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending' CHECK (onboarding_state IN ('pending','welcomed','seeded','complete')), paused boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false, updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(), PRIMARY KEY (user_id, bot_id))`. The `onboarding_state` CHECK mirrors `users.onboarding_state` from `0005_plan6_ops.sql`.
3. **Backfill** `user_bot_state` from `users.onboarding_state` for `bot_id='mediator'`:
   ```sql
   INSERT INTO user_bot_state (user_id, bot_id, onboarding_state)
   SELECT id, 'mediator', onboarding_state FROM users
   ON CONFLICT (user_id, bot_id) DO NOTHING;
   ```
4. **Enable RLS + anon-deny**.

### Step 5: Migration `0023_nullable_scope_columns.sql` (`migrations/0023_nullable_scope_columns.sql`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** (all `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, all nullable, no defaults that would rewrite hot tables):
   - `messages`: `topic_id uuid REFERENCES topics(id)`, `bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id)`.
   - `bot_turns`: `topic_id uuid REFERENCES topics(id)`, `bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id)`, plus audit cols `bot_spec_version text`, `hot_context_builder_version text`, `tool_schema_version text`.
   - `scheduled_jobs`: `topic_id uuid REFERENCES topics(id)`, `bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id)`.
   - `feedback`: `topic_id uuid REFERENCES topics(id)`, `bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id)`.
   - `bridge_candidates`: `topic_id uuid REFERENCES topics(id)`, `bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id)`, `dyad_id uuid REFERENCES dyads(id)`.
   - Artifact tables (`memories`, `themes`, `observations`, `watch_items`, `out_of_bounds`, and `distillations` from `0015_distillations.sql`): `recorded_by_bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id)`.
2. **Confirm** no `NOT NULL` is introduced anywhere in this migration; DoD requires zero new NOT NULLs. The executor should run `\d+` on each altered table post-migration and verify every new column shows no NOT NULL constraint.

### Step 6: Migration `0024_backfill.sql` + resumable runner (`migrations/0024_backfill.sql`, `scripts/backfill_artifact_topics.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Create** progress table inside `0024`:
   ```sql
   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS migration_progress (
     table_name text PRIMARY KEY,
     last_id uuid,
     backfill_started_at timestamptz,
     completed_at timestamptz
   );
   ```
2. **Author** `scripts/backfill_artifact_topics.py` (asyncpg) that iterates the artifact tables `['memories','themes','observations','watch_items','out_of_bounds','distillations']`. For each table, in a three-phase loop:
   - **Phase A — record start time**: `UPDATE migration_progress SET backfill_started_at = now() WHERE table_name = $1` (insert if missing). Read it back as `$started_at`.
   - **Phase B — UUID-cursor batch insert** (covers all rows existing at start): read `last_id` from `migration_progress` (default `'00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000'`). Loop:
     ```sql
     INSERT INTO artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id, tagged_by_bot_id, status, created_at)
     SELECT $1, a.id, (SELECT id FROM topics WHERE slug='relationship'), 'mediator', 'active', now()
     FROM <table> a
     WHERE a.id > $2
     ORDER BY a.id
     LIMIT 10000
     ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING
     RETURNING artifact_id;
     ```
     Update `migration_progress.last_id` to `max(returned artifact_id)` or keep prior value if zero rows, then mark `completed_at` if zero rows.
   - **Phase C — catch-up pass** (covers rows inserted during backfill): after Phase B completes (zero rows returned), run:
     ```sql
     INSERT INTO artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id, tagged_by_bot_id, status, created_at)
     SELECT $1, a.id, (SELECT id FROM topics WHERE slug='relationship'), 'mediator', 'active', now()
     FROM <table> a
     WHERE a.created_at >= $2
       AND NOT EXISTS (
         SELECT 1 FROM artifact_topics at2
         WHERE at2.artifact_table = $1 AND at2.artifact_id = a.id
       )
     ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING;
     ```
     This guarantees zero missed rows on a hot DB because: any row inserted during backfill either was picked up by UUID cursor (if its UUID sorted above the cursor at that moment) or is caught here. The `created_at >= $started_at` clause bounds the catch-up scan to recent rows.
   - **Advisory lock** the loop key `pg_try_advisory_lock(42424242 + table_index)` so concurrent runners don't double-insert. Sleep configurable 0–100ms between batches.
3. **Commit** validation SQL to `migrations/validation/0024_artifact_topics_counts.sql`:
   - One `SELECT count(*) FROM artifact_topics WHERE artifact_table='<t>'` per table, plus the equivalent `SELECT count(*) FROM <t>`. The script prints a side-by-side comparison.
   - **OOB validation gate** (must return 0): `SELECT count(*) FROM out_of_bounds WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM artifact_topics WHERE artifact_table='out_of_bounds' AND artifact_id=out_of_bounds.id)`.
   - Identity-backfill gate: `SELECT count(*) FROM user_identities WHERE transport='legacy'` must equal `SELECT count(*) FROM users WHERE phone IS NOT NULL`.
4. **Document** the run order in `migrations/validation/README.md`: how to run the backfill script, which zero-result queries gate the sprint, and the semantic-diff production check (see Step 11).

## Phase 2: Code skeletons (parallel to Phase 1 once tables exist in dev DB)

### Step 7: Extend `TurnContext` (`app/services/turn_context.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Change** `partner: User` → `partner: User | None`. This is a **type-only annotation change** — no default value, field stays in its current position (4th field). This is exactly what §16.1 specifies ("Make `partner: User | None`") and correctly preserves all ~30 positional constructors across `agentic.py:725`, `tests/test_tools.py`, `tests/test_turn_plan.py`, `tests/test_consult_perspective.py`, `tests/test_evals_capture.py`, `tests/test_send_outbound.py`, `tests/test_llm_phase.py`, and `app/services/tools/consult_perspective.py`. Since `triggering_message_ids` follows `partner` with no default, Python dataclass rules require `partner` to also have no default — which it won't.
2. **Add** new optional fields with sensible defaults that match mediator behavior. All fields go AFTER the existing required fields and `triggering_message_ids`, where defaults are legal:
   ```python
   bot_id: str | None = None
   bot_spec: Any | None = None  # BotSpec; Any avoids import cycle with app.bots.base
   binding_id: UUID | None = None
   participants_shape: Literal["solo", "dyad"] | None = None
   primary_topic_id: UUID | None = None
   primary_topic_slug: str | None = None
   channel_id: UUID | None = None
   read_scopes: Any | None = None   # ReadScopes
   write_scopes: Any | None = None  # WriteScopes
   cross_topic_policy: str | None = None
   ```
3. **Do not** touch `partner_of` or any caller. `agentic.py:686` calls `partner_of(pool, user)` without try/except — this is a latent robustness gap, but correcting it is out of scope for S1 (S1 leaves all call sites untouched). The type-only change from `User` to `User | None` is safe because all callers still pass a real `User` object at every construction site.

### Step 8: Extend `BotSpec` + add scope dataclasses (`app/bots/base.py`, `app/bots/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `ReadScopes` and `WriteScopes` frozen dataclasses matching the architecture doc §6.3 shape exactly:
   ```python
   @dataclass(frozen=True)
   class ReadScopes:
       topics: frozenset[str] | Literal["own", "all"] = "own"
       allow_cross_topic_peek: bool = True
       allow_cross_topic_status_injection: bool = True

   @dataclass(frozen=True)
   class WriteScopes:
       topics: frozenset[str] = field(default_factory=lambda: frozenset({"relationship"}))
       require_reason_for_cross_topic: bool = False
   ```
   This avoids the rename risk flagged in critique: `allow_cross_topic_status_injection` is `bool` per §6.3, not a `Literal['forbidden','peek','all']` triple. S4 extends these fields without breaking type changes.
2. **Extend** `BotSpec` (frozen dataclass) with optional fields, mediator-shaped defaults:
   ```python
   primary_topic_slug: str | None = None
   participants_shape: Literal["solo", "dyad"] | None = None
   read_scopes: ReadScopes = field(default_factory=ReadScopes)
   write_scopes: WriteScopes = field(default_factory=WriteScopes)
   cross_topic_policy: str | None = None
   bot_spec_version: str | None = None
   hot_context_builder_version: str | None = None
   tool_schema_version: str | None = None
   ```
   Since `read_scopes` and `write_scopes` use `default_factory`, all fields after them must also have defaults. The existing non-default fields (`bot_id`, `prompt_renderer`, `step_instructions`) precede these new fields, so this is safe. Verify field ordering compiles before declaring done.
3. **Export** `ReadScopes` and `WriteScopes` from `app/bots/__init__.py`:
   ```python
   from app.bots.base import BotSpec, ReadScopes, WriteScopes
   from app.bots.registry import BOT_SPECS, UnknownBotSpec, get_bot_spec
   __all__ = ["BOT_SPECS", "BotSpec", "ReadScopes", "WriteScopes", "UnknownBotSpec", "get_bot_spec"]
   ```

### Step 9: New routing module (`app/services/routing.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `app/services/routing.py` with a `BindingResolution` NamedTuple and three async functions:
   ```python
   from typing import NamedTuple

   class BindingResolution(NamedTuple):
       binding_id: UUID
       bot_id: str
       participants_shape: Literal["solo", "dyad"]
       dyad_id: UUID | None
       user_id: UUID | None
   ```
2. **`resolve_bot(pool, *, transport, address, guild_id=None, channel_id=None) -> str | None`**:
   ```sql
   SELECT bot_id FROM channels
   WHERE transport = $1 AND address = $2
     AND COALESCE(guild_id, '') = COALESCE($3, '')
     AND COALESCE(channel_id, '') = COALESCE($4, '')
   LIMIT 1;
   ```
3. **`resolve_sender(pool, *, transport, address) -> UUID | None`**:
   ```sql
   SELECT user_id FROM user_identities
   WHERE transport = $1 AND address = $2;
   ```
4. **`resolve_binding(pool, *, bot_id, user_id) -> BindingResolution | None`** — find a `bot_bindings` row whose `user_id = $2` (solo binding) OR whose `dyad_id` contains `$2` via `dyad_members` (dyad binding):
   ```sql
   SELECT bb.id AS binding_id, bb.bot_id,
          CASE WHEN bb.user_id IS NOT NULL THEN 'solo' ELSE 'dyad' END AS participants_shape,
          bb.dyad_id, bb.user_id
   FROM bot_bindings bb
   LEFT JOIN dyad_members dm ON dm.dyad_id = bb.dyad_id AND dm.user_id = $2
   WHERE bb.bot_id = $1
     AND (bb.user_id = $2 OR dm.user_id IS NOT NULL)
   LIMIT 1;
   ```
   The XOR check on `bot_bindings` guarantees exactly one of `user_id`/`dyad_id` is set, so `participants_shape` derivation is deterministic.
5. **Do not** import this module from `inbound.py` or `messaging.py`. S1 deliberately doesn't wire it.
6. **Write** unit tests in `tests/test_routing.py` using `FakePool` (extend it minimally to store channels/user_identities/bot_bindings rows). Seed the fake tables, assert each function returns the expected row and `None` on miss.

### Step 10: Wire `MEDIATOR_BOT` to DB-loaded fields (`app/bots/registry.py`, `app/main.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `async def populate_mediator_spec_from_db(pool)` to `app/bots/registry.py`. It:
   - Queries `bots` for `SELECT * FROM bots WHERE id='mediator'`.
   - If the row exists, reconstructs `MEDIATOR_BOT` via `MediatorBotSpec(bot_id=..., ...)` passing the DB-populated fields (`primary_topic_slug='relationship'`, `participants_shape='dyad'`, read_scopes, write_scopes, etc.) alongside the existing code-defined fields (`prompt_renderer`, `step_instructions`).
   - Stores the result: `BOT_SPECS['mediator'] = new_spec` (NOT `BOT_REGISTRY` — the module-level variable is named `BOT_SPECS` per `registry.py:8`).
   - If `bots` is empty (pre-migration), logs a warning and continues with code-defined defaults.
2. **Call** the helper from `app/main.py`'s `lifespan`, after `agentic.set_pool(pool)` and before `_configure_coalescer`. The exact insertion point:
   ```python
   # main.py lifespan, after agentic.set_pool(pool) (~line 167):
   from app.bots.registry import populate_mediator_spec_from_db
   await populate_mediator_spec_from_db(pool)
   ```
   This is the only async hook available — `main.py` never imports `registry.py` at module level, so the DB-populated spec must be applied after the pool exists but before any agentic turn fires. The coalescer is configured next, and the first inbound turn won't arrive until `discord.catch_up_recent_messages` or a webhook fires — both after `yield` in the lifespan.
3. **Keep** existing call sites untouched: `mediator.py:99` and `registry.py:9` still produce a usable spec without the DB call, so a cold start without migrations doesn't crash.

## Phase 3: Verification

### Step 11: Rendering-equality verification (`tests/test_hot_context_s1_invariance.py` — staging only; unit-level compile check in CI)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Acknowledge infrastructure gap:** The existing `tests/conftest.py` provides `FakePool` — an in-memory Python dict mock that hardcodes pattern matching for known SQL strings. It cannot execute raw DDL migrations (`CREATE TABLE`, `ALTER TABLE`, `INSERT ... SELECT` backfill statements). The `async_client` fixture monkeypatches `asyncpg.create_pool` to return `FakePool`. Without a real PostgreSQL test fixture, byte-equal rendering-equality verification between pre- and post-migration DB states is **not feasible in CI**.
2. **Two-tier verification approach:**
   - **CI-level (unit):** A basic compile-and-defaults test in `tests/test_s1_code_shape.py` that:
     - Constructs `TurnContext` with default values and asserts the new fields have the expected `None` defaults.
     - Constructs `BotSpec` (mediator) and asserts `ReadScopes`/`WriteScopes` defaults match mediator expectations.
     - Constructs `ReadScopes()` and `WriteScopes()` and asserts field values.
     - Runs `test_routing.py` unit tests against an extended `FakePool`.
   - **Staging-level (integration):** After applying migrations 0020–0024 to a staging clone with production-like data:
     1. Freeze `now` to a fixed UTC instant (via `unittest.mock.patch` on `datetime.now`).
     2. Build a deterministic hot context render from the pre-migration DB schema.
     3. Apply migrations 0020–0024 in-order idempotently.
     4. Rebuild hot context render from the post-migration state.
     5. Assert byte-equal rendered output between passes. The migrations are additive and writes don't reference new columns, so the rendered output must be identical.
     6. **Note:** Live `bot_turns.prompt_snapshot` hashes are not comparable — hot context embeds `now_utc` and relative time labels per `hot_context.py:65, 592` and `time_context.py:98`. Production check is **semantic diff on section counts/order/ids**, not prompt-hash equality.
3. **Document** the staging-only nature of the rendering-equality check in `migrations/validation/README.md`.

### Step 12: Run the full test suite
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `uv run pytest tests/test_routing.py tests/test_s1_code_shape.py -q` first (cheapest, new code).
2. **Run** `uv run pytest -q` (full suite). Expectation: zero regressions because no call site reads the new columns/fields and `TurnContext.partner` keeps the required semantics (type-only annotation change).
3. **If failures exist**, confirm they are pre-existing (from the unstaged dirty files listed in the brief) by comparing against `git stash` baseline: `git stash && uv run pytest -q && git stash pop`.

### Step 13: Mid-sprint checkpoint queries (`migrations/validation/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Author** `migrations/validation/s1_checkpoint.sql` containing the §16.1 checkpoint queries verbatim:
   - `SELECT count(*) FROM artifact_topics` vs sum of per-artifact counts.
   - `SELECT count(*) FROM messages WHERE bot_id IS NULL` — informational (must equal pre-backfill count since S1 doesn't write `bot_id`).
   - `SELECT count(*) FROM user_identities WHERE transport='legacy'` equals `SELECT count(*) FROM users WHERE phone IS NOT NULL`.
   - The OOB-classification zero-result gate.
2. **Document** "if mediator turn fails post-migration with no code changes, the migration isn't additive — find the FK/trigger and stop" in `migrations/validation/README.md`.

## Execution Order
1. Snapshot schema + audit `users.phone` semantics (Step 1).
2. Apply 0020 → 0021 → 0022 → 0023 in order on staging (Steps 2–5); each one stands alone.
3. Run `scripts/seed_channels.py` after 0020 is applied (Step 2b).
4. Run the `0024` resumable backfill script with catch-up pass (Step 6) against staging; gate on validation queries.
5. In parallel with (1)–(4), land code changes (Steps 7–10) on a feature branch — they don't touch call sites, so they can ship before the migrations are in prod.
6. Frozen-fixture rendering-equality test (Step 11) on staging with real PostgreSQL after migrations apply.
7. Unit tests + full pytest (Step 12).
8. Checkpoint queries (Step 13) committed alongside `0024`.

## Validation Order
1. Cheapest first: `pytest tests/test_routing.py tests/test_s1_code_shape.py` — new code isolated, fast, FakePool-based.
2. `pytest -q` full suite — regression check; expect green (compare against stash baseline if needed).
3. Apply migrations to staging clone of prod; run `scripts/seed_channels.py`; run `scripts/backfill_artifact_topics.py`; run `migrations/validation/s1_checkpoint.sql`. **All gates must return 0 / equal counts.**
4. Manual mediator turn on staging post-migration with unchanged code; if it fails, halt and find the FK/trigger that breaks additivity (§16.1 risk).
5. Frozen-fixture rendering-equality check on staging (byte-equal pre/post migration hot context render).

    "idea": "# Sprint 1 \u2014 Foundation schema + code shape\n\n## Orchestration context\n\nYou are executing **Sprint 1** of the 7-sprint multi-agent architecture buildout for the Veas codebase. Vendor inherits from `~/.config/megaplan/config.json` (Claude on plan/revise/gate/finalize, Codex on critique/execute/review).\n\n## Locked decisions \u2014 do NOT re-debate during plan/critique\n\nThese two decisions are closed per \u00a716.8 and the architecture-doc author. The \u00a716.1 validation query (`SELECT count(*) FROM out_of_bounds WHERE NOT EXISTS (...)` = 0) is the gate.\n\nIf the plan phase emits sub-tasks that re-open either of these, treat that as a planner regression \u2014 they are inputs, not questions.\n\n## Reference reading (for context, not for re-deciding)\n\n- `migrations/0001_init.sql`, `0013_bridge_candidates.sql`, `0015_distillations.sql`, `0017_scheduled_tasks.sql` \u2014 confirm existing column shapes against \u00a73 of the architecture doc.\n- `app/services/inbound.py:23, 136, 161` and `app/services/messaging.py:55, 84, 155, 338, 370` \u2014 current scope sites.\n- \u00a73, \u00a77, \u00a710, \u00a712.2 of the architecture doc for the conceptual model behind the migrations.\n\nThe full architecture doc (1251 lines) is at the path above. Read what is useful; do not paste it into the plan.\n\n---\n\n## Sprint 1 \u2014 Foundation schema + code shape (\u00a716.1 verbatim)\n\n**Goal.** Land every additive table, backfill, and the code skeletons (`TurnContext` fields, `BotSpec` carrier fields, channel/binding/identity resolution helpers) so subsequent sprints only flip switches.\n\n**Pre-flight context.**\n\n- Read `migrations/0001_init.sql`, `0013_bridge_candidates.sql`, `0015_distillations.sql`, `0017_scheduled_tasks.sql` to confirm column shapes against \u00a73.\n- Read `app/services/inbound.py:23, 136, 161` and `app/services/messaging.py:55, 84, 155, 338, 370`.\n- Confirm `users.phone` backfill semantics for legacy Discord ids: `transport='legacy'` per \u00a712.2.\n- Snapshot prod schema (`pg_dump --schema-only`) for rollback comparison.\n\n**Work items, ordered.**\n\n1. Migration `0020_topics_bots_bindings.sql`: `topics`, `bots`, `dyads`, `dyad_members`, `bot_bindings`, `channels`, `user_identities`. Seed `relationship` topic, `mediator` bot, one `dyads` row, two `dyad_members`, one `bot_bindings`, channel rows for current Discord + WhatsApp. Backfill `user_identities` from `users.phone`.\n2. Migration `0021_artifact_topics.sql`: create the join table + partial indexes from \u00a72. No FK to each artifact table (polymorphic via `artifact_table` column) \u2014 orphan prevention is application discipline; lint catches it in S2a.\n3. Migration `0022_topic_status_user_bot_state.sql`: `topic_status` (both partial indexes from \u00a77), `user_bot_state` populated from `users.onboarding_state` for `mediator`.\n4. Migration `0023_nullable_scope_columns.sql`: nullable `topic_id`, `bot_id` on `messages`, `bot_turns`, `scheduled_jobs`, `feedback`, `bridge_candidates`; `dyad_id` on `bridge_candidates`; `recorded_by_bot_id` on artifact tables; audit columns on `bot_turns` (`bot_spec_version`, `hot_context_builder_version`, `tool_schema_version`).\n5. Migration `0024_backfill.sql`: **cursor-keyed `INSERT INTO artifact_topics SELECT ... WHERE id > last_id ORDER BY id LIMIT 10000` loops with resumability**, per artifact table. Not UPDATEs (the join table is new and empty). Track progress in a small `migration_progress(table, last_id)` table that can resume on restart. Validation queries committed to `migrations/validation/`.\n\n   **OOB backfill rule (resolves \u00a719/\u00a720 ambiguity):** existing `out_of_bounds` rows are all classified as **`relationship` topic-scoped** \u2014 they get an `artifact_topics` row pointing at the relationship topic. \"No `artifact_topics` rows = global OOB\" is a *new mode introduced for the future*; no existing rows use it. Validation query: `SELECT count(*) FROM out_of_bounds WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM artifact_topics WHERE artifact_table='out_of_bounds' AND artifact_id=out_of_bounds.id)` must be zero post-backfill.\n6. Code: extend `app/services/turn_context.py` with `bot_id`, `bot_spec`, `binding_id`, `participants_shape`, `primary_topic_id`, `primary_topic_slug`, `channel_id`, `read_scopes`, `write_scopes`, `cross_topic_policy` \u2014 **all optional with defaults**. Make `partner: User | None`. Existing dereference sites untouched; defaults keep `partner` non-None.\n7. Code: extend `app/bots/base.py` BotSpec with the new fields as optional with mediator-shaped defaults. Add `ReadScopes` / `WriteScopes` dataclasses. No call-site changes.\n8. Code: new module `app/services/routing.py` \u2014 `resolve_bot(event)`, `resolve_sender(transport, address)`, `resolve_binding(bot_id, user_id)`. **Not wired into inbound yet.** Unit tests cover them.\n9. Code: `MEDIATOR_BOT` carries new fields populated from DB at startup.\n\nDependencies: (1) gates (2,3,4); (4) gates (5); (6\u20139) parallel to migrations as long as no call sites change.\n\n**Mid-sprint checkpoint (day 5\u20136).**\n\n- All migrations applied to staging clone of prod with full row counts.\n- `SELECT count(*) FROM artifact_topics` equals sum of (memories + themes + observations + watch_items + distillations + out_of_bounds) row counts.\n- `SELECT count(*) FROM messages WHERE bot_id IS NULL` is zero on the backfilled set.\n- `SELECT count(*) FROM user_identities WHERE transport='legacy'` equals `count(*) FROM users WHERE phone IS NOT NULL`.\n- Mediator runs against the migrated staging DB with **no code changes** and a clean turn completes end-to-end. **If this fails, the additive migration isn't actually additive \u2014 stop and find the FK or trigger that broke.**\n\n**Definition of done.**\n\n- All migrations live in prod. Backfill validated by the OOB query above and equivalent counts per artifact table.\n- Mediator behavior verified via **frozen-fixture rendering equality**: deterministic snapshot of DB + frozen `now` \u2192 render hot context pre- and post-migration \u2192 byte-equal. Live `bot_turns.prompt_snapshot` hashes are not comparable (hot context embeds `now_utc` and relative time labels per `hot_context.py:65, 592` and `time_context.py:98`); production check is **semantic diff on section counts/order/ids**, not prompt-hash equality.\n- `TurnContext`, `BotSpec`, `routing.py` shipped with new fields populated by default constructors. No call site references the new fields yet.\n- Zero new NOT NULL constraints.\n\n**Risks.**\n\n- Backfilling `artifact_topics` on a hot DB is the largest write of the sprint. Batch + advisory-lock or accept lag. Schedule off-hours.\n- `users.phone` \u2192 `user_identities` backfill: if any row contains both a phone and a Discord id, both must become identity rows. Audit before backfill.\n- `bot_bindings` CHECK constraint mistake (`(user_id IS NOT NULL) <> (dyad_id IS NOT NULL)`) is one typo from blocking inserts.         "id": "issue_hints-2",
        "concern": "Did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: The 0024 backfill Phase B uses `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING artifact_id` to advance `migration_progress.last_id`. If a batch of rows was already inserted but `last_id` was not updated (e.g., because the runner crashed between the INSERT commit and the UPDATE, or because they ran in separate implicit transactions), the next run returns zero rows and the plan instructs the executor to mark `completed_at`, permanently skipping all remaining rows in that table. The plan never specifies that the INSERT and the UPDATE must execute inside the same explicit transaction, which is required for this cursor scheme to be safe.",

        "resolution": "Addressed all 21 significant flags from the gate critique. Major fixes: (1) Corrected the pre-flight audit to acknowledge that users.phone stores both phone numbers and Discord IDs \u2014 backfill as transport='legacy' remains correct per locked decision. (2) Fixed TurnContext.partner change to be type-only (`User | None` with NO default) to avoid Python dataclass ordering violations, preserving all 30+ positional constructors. (3) Replaced UUID-only backfill cursor with a three-phase approach (record start time, UUID-cursor batch insert, catch-up pass on `created_at >= start_time`) to guarantee zero missed rows on a hot DB. (4) Switched channel seeding from a non-numeric `0020a` pseudo-migration to a runtime bootstrap script `scripts/seed_channels.py`. (5) Matched ReadScopes/WriteScopes field types to architecture doc \u00a76.3 exactly (`allow_cross_topic_status_injection: bool`, not Literal). (6) Defined explicit BindingResolution NamedTuple and concrete SQL for `resolve_binding`. (7) Named the MEDIATOR_BOT startup hook precisely (`main.py` lifespan after `agentic.set_pool`, calling `populate_mediator_spec_from_db` from registry). (8) Acknowledged FakePool limitation; split rendering-equality verification into CI-level compile/defaults test and staging-only integration test. (9) Made dyad seeding conditionally skip (RAISE NOTICE, not EXCEPTION) for CI safety. (10) Fixed BOT_REGISTRY \u2192 BOT_SPECS. (11) Added ReadScopes/WriteScopes exports from `app/bots/__init__.py`."

Option (a) is not mentioned; option (b) breaks every positional constructor in the repo, including the critical production site `app/services/agentic.py:725-746` (`TurnContext(turn_id, active_pool, user, partner, triggering_message_ids, ...)`), and numerous test sites in `tests/test_tools.py` that pass `partner` positionally as the 4th argument.       {
        "id": "correctness-2",
        "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: UUID cursor-keyed backfill on a hot DB will miss concurrent inserts. The plan uses `WHERE id > last_id ORDER BY id LIMIT 10000` on tables whose PKs are `gen_random_uuid()` (random v4 UUIDs). Because UUIDs are not monotonic with insertion time, any new row inserted during the backfill whose UUID sorts lexicographically below the current cursor will be skipped. The `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and advisory lock prevent duplicate work from concurrent backfill runners, but they do not stop the application from inserting new artifacts. Since S1 does not yet write `artifact_topics` rows at insert time, those missed rows will remain orphaned forever, causing the post-backfill validation count query (`count(*) FROM artifact_topics` vs sum of artifact counts) to fail. A composite cursor on `(created_at, id)` or a quiesced backfill window is needed for correctness on a hot database.",

(4) Switched channel seeding from a non-numeric `0020a` pseudo-migration to a runtime bootstrap script `scripts/seed_channels.py`. (5) Matched ReadScopes/WriteScopes field types to architecture doc \u00a76.3 exactly (`allow_cross_topic_status_injection: bool`, not Literal).         "resolution": "Addressed all 21 significant flags from the gate critique. Major fixes: (1) Corrected the pre-flight audit to acknowledge that users.phone stores both phone numbers and Discord IDs \u2014 backfill as transport='legacy' remains correct per locked decision. (2) Fixed TurnContext.partner change to be type-only (`User | None` with NO default) to avoid Python dataclass ordering violations, preserving all 30+ positional constructors. If a batch of rows was already inserted but `last_id` was not updated (e.g., because the runner crashed between the INSERT commit and the UPDATE, or because they ran in separate implicit transactions), the next run returns zero rows and the plan instructs the executor to mark `completed_at`, permanently skipping all remaining rows in that table. The plan never specifies that the INSERT and the UPDATE must execute inside the same explicit transaction, which is required for this cursor scheme to be safe.",

        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Verify that the plan remains aligned with the user's original intent, not just internal plan quality.
        - Remove unjustified scope growth. If critique raised scope creep, narrow the plan back to the original idea unless the broader work is strictly required.
        - Maintain the structural template: H1 title, ## Overview, phase sections with numbered step sections, ## Execution Order or ## Validation Order.
        - CRITICAL: Your entire revised plan markdown (all sections) must be output as the `plan` field in the structured output. The prose response must not contain the plan text.
        - CRITICAL: Return only the structured JSON object for the schema fields `plan`, `changes_summary`, `flags_addressed`, `assumptions`, `success_criteria`, and `questions`. Do not add commentary before or after the JSON object.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

For each table in `['memories','themes','observations','watch_items','out_of_bounds','distillations']`:\n   - Acquire `pg_advisory_lock(42424242 + table_index)`; release on exit.\n   - **Phase A — record start time** (single transaction): upsert `migration_progress` row, set `backfill_started_at=now()` if NULL. CRITICAL: each batch's INSERT and the matching `migration_progress.last_id` UPDATE must execute inside the SAME explicit transaction** (`async with conn.transaction():`). Pseudocode:\n     ```python\n     async with conn.transaction():\n         rows = await conn.fetch(\n             \"\"\"\n             INSERT INTO artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id, tagged_by_bot_id, status, created_at)\n             SELECT $1, a.id, (SELECT id FROM topics WHERE slug='relationship'), 'mediator', 'active', now()\n             FROM <table> a WHERE a.id > $2 ORDER BY a.id LIMIT 10000\n             ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING\n             RETURNING artifact_id\n             \"\"\", table_name, last_id)\n         if not rows:\n             # also re-fetch source rows above last_id without ON CONFLICT filter\n             # to distinguish \"all done\" from \"all already inserted\"\n             remaining = await conn.fetchval(\n                 \"SELECT count(*) FROM <table> WHERE id > $1\", last_id)\n             if remaining == 0:\n                 await conn.execute(\n                     \"UPDATE migration_progress SET last_id=$2 WHERE table_name=$1\",\n                     table_name, last_id)\n                 break  # proceed to Phase C\n             # else: advance cursor by max id of the skipped batch\n             new_last = await conn.fetchval(\n                 \"SELECT max(id) FROM (SELECT id FROM <table> WHERE id > $1 ORDER BY id LIMIT 10000) s\",\n                 last_id)\n             await conn.execute(\n                 \"UPDATE migration_progress SET last_id=$2 WHERE table_name=$1\",\n                 table_name, new_last)\n             last_id = new_last\n             continue\n         new_last = max(r['artifact_id'] for r in rows)\n         await conn.execute(\n             \"UPDATE migration_progress SET last_id=$2 WHERE table_name=$1\",\n             table_name, new_last)\n         last_id = new_last\n     ```\n     The single-transaction guarantee makes `(inserted rows, advanced cursor)` atomic — a crash leaves both committed or neither, never the \"advanced past uninserted rows\" failure mode flagged in the gate. The `remaining == 0` check distinguishes \"done\" from \"current batch's rows already existed\" so the loop never stalls or falsely terminates.\n   - **Phase C — catch-up pass** for rows inserted during backfill (atomic with `completed_at`):\n     ```python\n     async with conn.transaction():\n         await conn.execute(\"\"\"\n           INSERT INTO artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id, tagged_by_bot_id, status, created_at)\n           SELECT $1, a.id, (SELECT id FROM topics WHERE slug='relationship'), 'mediator', 'active', now()\n           FROM <table> a\n           WHERE a.created_at >= $2\n             AND NOT EXISTS (\n               SELECT 1 FROM artifact_topics at2\n               WHERE at2.artifact_table = $1 AND at2.artifact_id = a.id)\n           ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING\n         \"\"\", table_name, started_at)\n         await conn.execute(\n             \"UPDATE migration_progress SET completed_at=now() WHERE table_name=$1\",\n             table_name)\n     ```\n   - Sleep 0–100ms (configurable) between Phase B batches.\n3. **Commit** `migrations/validation/0024_artifact_topics_counts.sql`: per-table `count(*) FROM artifact_topics WHERE artifact_table='<t>'` vs `count(*) FROM <t>`; OOB zero-result gate; `user_identities` legacy-count gate.\n4. **Document** in `migrations/validation/README.md`: run order; gate queries; the `messages.bot_id IS NULL` informational note (see Step 13).\n\n## Phase 2: Code skeletons\n\n### Step 7: Extend `TurnContext` (`app/services/turn_context.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Change** `partner: User` → `partner: User | None` (type-only annotation; NO default; field stays at position 4). (Without the matching expression index in 0020, this would fail; Step 2 adds it.)\n3. **Document** in `scripts/README.md` that the script is post-migration, idempotent, and that missing credentials are OK.\n\n### Step 3: Migration `0021_artifact_topics.sql` (`migrations/0021_artifact_topics.sql`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Create** `artifact_topics` per §2: PK `(artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id)`, status default `'active'` CHECK `IN ('active','retired')`, FKs to `topics(id)` and `bots(id)` for `tagged_by_bot_id`, `reason text`, `retired_at timestamptz`. Both partial indexes filtered on `status='active'`.\n2. **Enable RLS + anon-deny**.\n\n### Step 4: Migration `0022_topic_status_user_bot_state.sql` (`migrations/0022_topic_status_user_bot_state.sql`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. **Create** `topic_status` per §7 — **including surrogate PK `id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid()`** (omitted in prior revision; S2+ code assumes it). Full DDL:\n   ```sql\n   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS topic_status (\n     id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n     topic_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES topics(id),\n     dyad_id uuid REFERENCES dyads(id),\n     user_id uuid REFERENCES users(id),\n     headline text NOT NULL,\n     body text NOT NULL,\n     last_updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),\n     last_updated_by_bot_id text NOT NULL REFERENCES bots(id),\n     CHECK ((user_id IS NOT NULL) <> (dyad_id IS NOT NULL))\n   );\n   CREATE UNIQUE INDEX topic_status_user_key ON topic_status (topic_id, user_id) WHERE user_id IS NOT NULL;\n   CREATE UNIQUE INDEX topic_status_dyad_key ON topic_status (topic_id, dyad_id) WHERE dyad_id IS NOT NULL;\n   ```\n2. **Create** `user_bot_state(user_id uuid REFERENCES users(id), bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id), onboarding_state text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending' CHECK (onboarding_state IN ('pending','welcomed','seeded','complete')), paused boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false, updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(), PRIMARY KEY (user_id, bot_id))`.\n3. **Backfill** `user_bot_state` from `users.onboarding_state` for `bot_id='mediator'` with `ON CONFLICT (user_id, bot_id) DO NOTHING`.\n4. **Enable RLS + anon-deny**.\n\n### Step 5: Migration `0023_nullable_scope_columns.sql` (`migrations/0023_nullable_scope_columns.sql`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. **Add** (all `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, all nullable):\n   - `messages`, `bot_turns`, `scheduled_jobs`, `feedback`: `topic_id uuid REFERENCES topics(id)`, `bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id)`.\n   - `bot_turns` also: `bot_spec_version text`, `hot_context_builder_version text`, `tool_schema_version text`.\n   - `bridge_candidates`: `topic_id`, `bot_id`, `dyad_id uuid REFERENCES dyads(id)`.\n   - Artifact tables (`memories`, `themes`, `observations`, `watch_items`, `out_of_bounds`, `distillations`): `recorded_by_bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id)`.\n2. **Confirm** via `\\d+` on each touched table that no new column has `NOT NULL`.\n\n### Step 6: Migration `0024_backfill.sql` + resumable runner (`migrations/0024_backfill.sql`, `scripts/backfill_artifact_topics.py`)\n**Scope:** Large\n1. **Create** progress table in `0024`:\n   ```sql\n   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS migration_progress (\n     table_name text PRIMARY KEY,\n     last_id uuid,\n     backfill_started_at timestamptz,\n     completed_at timestamptz\n   );\n   ```\n2. **Author** `scripts/backfill_artifact_topics.py` (asyncpg). For each artifact table in `['memories','themes','observations','watch_items','out_of_bounds','distillations']`:\n   - **Phase A — record start time** (one-time, no-op if already set): `INSERT INTO migration_progress (table_name, backfill_started_at) VALUES ($1, now()) ON CONFLICT (table_name) DO UPDATE SET backfill_started_at = COALESCE(migration_progress.backfill_started_at, now()) RETURNING backfill_started_at;` Capture as `$started_at`.\n   - **Phase B — UUID-cursor batch insert, INSERT + UPDATE in ONE transaction.** Each batch iteration must execute inside a single explicit `async with conn.transaction():` block so the cursor advance is atomic with the inserts; otherwise a crash between commits permanently skips rows. Pseudocode (the transaction wrapper is REQUIRED):\n     ```python\n     while True:\n         async with conn.transaction():\n             rows = await conn.fetch(\n                 \"\"\"\n                 WITH ins AS (\n                   INSERT INTO artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id, tagged_by_bot_id, status, created_at)\n                   SELECT $1, a.id, (SELECT id FROM topics WHERE slug='relationship'),\n                          'mediator', 'active', now()\n                   FROM <table> a\n                   WHERE a.id > $2\n                   ORDER BY a.id\n                   LIMIT 10000\n                   ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING\n                   RETURNING artifact_id\n                 ),\n                 -- Also need the source-table cursor advance even when ON CONFLICT skipped everything\n                 src AS (\n                   SELECT a.id FROM <table> a WHERE a.id > $2 ORDER BY a.id LIMIT 10000\n                 )\n                 SELECT (SELECT max(id) FROM src) AS new_cursor,\n                        (SELECT count(*) FROM src) AS scanned\n                 \"\"\", table_name, last_id)\n             new_cursor, scanned = rows[0][\"new_cursor\"], rows[0][\"scanned\"]\n             if scanned == 0:\n                 break\n             await conn.execute(\n                 \"UPDATE migration_progress SET last_id=$2 WHERE table_name=$1\",\n                 table_name, new_cursor)\n             last_id = new_cursor\n     ```\n     Cursor advance is keyed off `src` (rows scanned), not `ins` (rows inserted), so a fully-conflicting batch still advances the cursor. The single `async with conn.transaction()` makes the insert and the cursor update commit together — no possibility of \"inserted but cursor not advanced\" on crash.\n   - **Phase C — catch-up pass** (covers rows whose UUID sorted below the cursor at insert time on a hot DB):\n     ```sql\n     INSERT INTO artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id, tagged_by_bot_id, status, created_at)\n     SELECT $1, a.id, (SELECT id FROM topics WHERE slug='relationship'), 'mediator', 'active', now()\n     FROM <table> a\n     WHERE a.created_at >= $2  -- $started_at\n       AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM artifact_topics at2\n                       WHERE at2.artifact_table=$1 AND at2.artifact_id=a.id)\n     ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING;\n     ```\n   - After Phase C, mark `completed_at = now()`.\n   - **Advisory lock** the runner key `pg_try_advisory_lock(42424242 + table_index)`. Sleep 0–100 ms between batches (configurable).\n3. **Commit** validation SQL to `migrations/validation/0024_artifact_topics_counts.sql`:\n   - Per-table count parity.\n   - OOB zero-result gate (must return 0): `SELECT count(*) FROM out_of_bounds WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM artifact_topics WHERE artifact_table='out_of_bounds' AND artifact_id=out_of_bounds.id)`.\n   - Identity gate: `count(*) FROM user_identities WHERE transport='legacy'` equals `count(*) FROM users WHERE phone IS NOT NULL`.\n4. **Document** in `migrations/validation/README.md`: run order, gate queries, and the §16.1 `messages.bot_id IS NULL` checkpoint deviation (see Step 13).\n\n## Phase 2: Code skeletons (parallel to Phase 1)\n\n### Step 7: Extend `TurnContext` (`app/services/turn_context.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Change** `partner: User` → `partner: User | None`. **Type-only annotation, no default, position 4 preserved.** All ~30 positional constructors continue to work because `triggering_message_ids` (position 5) still has no default.\n2. **Add** ten new optional fields AFTER `triggering_message_ids` with defaults (see plan v2 list). `bot_spec`, `read_scopes`, `write_scopes` typed `Any | None` to avoid import cycle with `app.bots.base`.\n3. **Do not** modify `partner_of` or any of the ~72 `ctx.partner.*` dereferences in `read_tools.py`, `write_tools.py`, `hot_context.py`, `staging.py`. Documented as accepted S5 follow-up in the Overview.\n\n### Step 8: Extend `BotSpec` + add scope dataclasses (`app/bots/base.py`, `app/bots/__init__.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Add** `ReadScopes` and `WriteScopes` frozen dataclasses matching §6.3 (`allow_cross_topic_status_injection: bool`, not Literal). See plan v2 shape.\n2. **Extend** `BotSpec` with eight optional fields (mediator defaults via `field(default_factory=...)`).\n3. **Export** both from `app/bots/__init__.py` `__all__`.\n4. **Do NOT** modify `BotSpec.render_system_prompt`'s `partner: User` parameter. Type mismatch with `TurnContext.partner: User | None` is acknowledged as accepted S5 follow-up in the Overview — S1 never passes `None` so no runtime regression.\n\n### Step 9: New routing module (`app/services/routing.py`, `tests/test_routing.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. **Create** `app/services/routing.py` with `BindingResolution` `NamedTuple` and three async functions (`resolve_bot`, `resolve_sender`, `resolve_binding`) — SQL bodies as in plan v2.\n2. **Do not** import this module from `inbound.py` or `messaging.py`.\n3. **Unit tests in `tests/test_routing.py` only — DO NOT modify `tests/conftest.py`.** Because `conftest.py` is on the locked dirty-files list, the FakePool extension required for routing must be a subclass or local helper defined inside `tests/test_routing.py`:\n   ```python\n   # tests/test_routing.py\n   from tests.conftest import FakePool  # read-only import\n\n   class RoutingFakePool(FakePool):\n       def __init__(self):\n           super().__init__()\n           self.channels = []\n           self.user_identities = []\n           self.bot_bindings = []\n           self.dyads = []\n           self.dyad_members = []\n       # Override fetch/fetchrow to pattern-match the three new SQL strings.\n   ```\n   No change to `conftest.py`. If `FakePool` lacks a hook the subclass can't extend cleanly, prefer module-level monkeypatching inside the test file over editing the conftest.\n\n### Step 10: Wire `MEDIATOR_BOT` to DB-loaded fields (`app/bots/registry.py`, `app/main.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Add** `async def populate_mediator_spec_from_db(pool)` to `app/bots/registry.py`. It reads only `display_name` from `SELECT display_name FROM bots WHERE id='mediator'` — all scope/topic/version fields are hardcoded mediator defaults in code, not DB-driven (the `bots` table is intentionally thin per §3). Reconstructs `MEDIATOR_BOT` and assigns to `BOT_SPECS['mediator']`. On miss/empty: warn and keep code defaults.\n2. **Call** from `app/main.py` lifespan after `agentic.set_pool(pool)` and before `_configure_coalescer`.\n3. **Keep** module-level `MEDIATOR_BOT` and `BOT_SPECS` initialization untouched — cold start without migrations must not crash.\n\n## Phase 3: Verification\n\n### Step 11: Rendering-equality verification (`tests/test_s1_code_shape.py` — CI; staging-only integration check documented)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. **CI-level test** in NEW file `tests/test_s1_code_shape.py` (does not touch `conftest.py` or any dirty test file):\n   - Construct `TurnContext` with the existing required positional args and assert each new field defaults to `None` (or the scope-dataclass default).\n   - Construct `BotSpec` and assert `ReadScopes`/`WriteScopes` default shapes.\n   - Import `app.bots.__init__` and assert `ReadScopes`/`WriteScopes` present in `__all__`.\n2. **Staging-level integration check** (documented in `migrations/validation/README.md`, executed by the human on staging — FakePool cannot run DDL):\n   - Freeze `now`, render hot context against pre-migration snapshot, apply 0020–0024 idempotently, re-render, assert byte-equal. Production check is semantic diff on `bot_turns.prompt_snapshot` section counts/order/ids (not hash equality — `hot_context.py:65, 592` and `time_context.py:98` embed `now_utc`).\n\n### Step 12: Run the test suite\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Run** `uv run pytest tests/test_routing.py tests/test_s1_code_shape.py -q` first.\n2. **Run** `uv run pytest -q` full suite. If failures appear, compare against `git stash` baseline (the dirty-files-list may already make the suite red pre-S1).\n\n### Step 13: Mid-sprint checkpoint queries (`migrations/validation/s1_checkpoint.sql`, `migrations/validation/README.md`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Author** `migrations/validation/s1_checkpoint.sql` with:\n   - `count(*) FROM artifact_topics` vs sum of per-artifact counts.\n   - `count(*) FROM user_identities WHERE transport='legacy'` vs `count(*) FROM users WHERE phone IS NOT NULL`.\n   - OOB zero-result gate.\n   - `count(*) FROM messages WHERE bot_id IS NULL` — **with documented divergence note**.\n2. **Document the `messages.bot_id IS NULL` divergence explicitly** in `migrations/validation/README.md`:\n   > The locked brief §16.1 lists `count(*) FROM messages WHERE bot_id IS NULL = 0` as a checkpoint. **S1 deliberately does not write `messages.bot_id`** (the column is added in 0023 as nullable; no insert site is touched per \"Deliberately not in this sprint\"). Sprint S2a is the first sprint that writes `bot_id` on inserts and the first sprint where this gate becomes meaningful.\n3. **Document** \"if mediator turn fails post-migration with no code changes, the migration isn't additive — stop and find the FK/trigger\" in the same README.\n\n## Execution Order\n1. Checkpoint queries (Step 13).\n\n## Validation Order\n1. `pytest tests/test_routing.py tests/test_s1_code_shape.py` — cheapest, FakePool-based.\n2. `pytest -q` — full suite (compare with stash baseline if needed).\n3.
